The bar: applied, caught and NOT APPLIED equal 0.4.8's per file (579/579/0), every control ok, the not-ok set empty, two runs with the plain package.json command, under 20 minutes. ## 08:35 — D412 part 2(a) (O, p-d412-part2a-O_2026-10-19.md, git-blob c6b9319e), read whole. One core-mutation mutant: copy about 100 ms (0.5%); inner whole run 20-22 s over 76 tests; narrowed to caughtBy 127-536 ms; caught in all 3. RULING, part 2(b): option 1, made safe in both directions, one commit per harness: core-mutation, then mutation.test.js, then core-close-mutation (measure one mutant of each first; skip if narrowing doesn't pay):
(i) the CONTROL runs whole as now, AND narrowed once per distinct caughtBy pattern. Each narrowed control must pass, which proves the named test passes alone on unmutated code. A pattern that fails alone keeps its mutants whole.
(ii) A mutant runs narrowed. Caught means caught. NOT caught re-runs whole, and the whole run's verdict stands. So narrowing can only speed up a catch, never weaken a verdict.
(iii) caughtBy is regex-escaped. A pattern matching a describe block or more than one test is flagged in the output.
(iv) The crash stand-ins (D352-D354) stay whole.
(v) Per-MUTANT comparison: each harness's mutant-by-mutant verdict ('applied, and caught by <name>') equals the 0.4.8 suite TAP's (3841908), not just the totals.
Then two full runs with the plain package.json command. The bar is unchanged (579/579/0 per file, every control ok, the not-ok set empty), with a target under 20 minutes. Option 2 (copy less) is dropped for core-mutation on the numbers. surfaced +1 (the fallback that makes narrowing one-directional).

## 14:51 — D407b status RULING (A, p-d407b-fix-A_2026-10-19.md, git-blob 39653cb0; d407b-a 17f4d5b on 18b8fd8, 13 files +310 -54, 0 keeper). Checked the row 4 amendment (release-frame.test.js diff 18b8fd8..17f4d5b): old cost = handler + SUM of every frame >0.5 ms over 120 ms; new = MAX(handler, any one frame from 20 ms before). A's reason holds: summing counts the ghost's deliberately sliced frames as one task, and the bar is one frame's. ACCEPTED in direction, said so by name in the test. CATCH (applies to rows 1, 6, 7 and the ABAB harness too, so to the accepted D407 release figures): max(handler, frame) leaves out that an input handler and the rAF callbacks of the frame it is dispatched into run in the SAME frame (Chrome dispatches input just before rAF), so a 4 ms handler + a 4 ms frame is an 8 ms frame that reads as 4. Condition: cost = max(handler + the frame it lands in, any later frame); row 4 must then be shown red on b306309 and the ABAB uses the same measure. Release handlers are now <1 ms (D407b), so the release rows should barely move; edit and place may. Also accepted: idle callbacks timed as tasks; A's own correction that D407's place/edit figures were understated for both trees (inventory note, not a re-ruling of D407). Window: A gets both slots for ~40 min after B's D410 timing and A's covering set, same terms as 12:58 (P starts no new D411 session, others start no heavy runs, A takes both slots then does the quiet check inside its hold). ## 09:11 — D430 from the keeper's 09:10 report: 'if a part of the equation is blank, it doesnt count as 0. if one is blank auto set it to 0'. Cause read at 6261a22 (not run): asTyped panel.js:208 sends an emptied field as '', and extendOptions' num (panel.js:59) maps '' to null, which sends no target. So an emptied turn, climb or bank continues at the head's value. Plan exo_memory/loop/plan_d430_blank_is_zero_2026-10-22.md, pane C, check it, test first. An emptied field counts as 0 and the box shows 0, for turn, climb, bank, cup, edge and tube. These keep their meaning: length and width (0 refused), grip (blank = head's grip by design), the right edge (blank follows left) and corner-mode turn/climb (blank is their rest state). Scoping is mine; the keeper may widen it. It rides 0.4.14 if accepted before D429 Part 3. surfaced +1.

## 23:24 — D437 P §3 (headless pass) read: exo_memory/handback/p-d437-P_2026-10-22.md §3, §8, §9. The keeper's crash reproduces in the page's own Export at both b6969da and 6261a22, 5 runs of 5, each with his stack line for line. It is e0000008 CALL_AND_RETRY_LAST at 2.25 GB of old space, while the 4 GB cage still has 278-413 MB free: the largest free region is only 23-36 MB, too small for soup()'s plain-array growth. The page holds 1.3-1.5 GB before the press. This agrees with E's 2a; E's headless Chrome run finishing in 34.9 s is that fix measured in the page, and accepted at 22:45. P's §8 RESULT-line correction is why E rebuilt the watcher. CHANGES THE NEXT STEP for B (2b): P's inferred point is that Chrome's desktop V8 shares one pointer-compression cage across the isolates in a process, so a worker in the same renderer may NOT escape the 4 GB. And a V8 OOM is fatal to the process, whatever isolate it is in. Ruled for B: the 2b guard row must force a REAL out-of-memory in the worker, not a throw, in headless Chrome (--mute-audio). It must show the page still drawn and the AC folder byte-identical. If the page dies, the worker does not count as the guard. Then B plans an out-of-process export (the Tauri side or a sidecar) or a bound that fails by name before the cage fills, and rings before building it. Measured, not assumed. P's NEXT lacked 'when'; filled. surfaced +1.

## 23:26 — D437 Part 2b (B, d437b-b, uncommitted) interim read whole: exo_memory/handback/p-d437b-B_2026-10-22.md. B's probes say a real V8 OOM in a bare worker kills the page in headless Chrome and in Edge 155; that confirms P's premise for process death and refutes the plan's 'own isolate, so a message' (my plan carried P's step 5 unchecked). The same OOM inside a worker in a sandbox='allow-scripts' iframe ends only that frame's process: the page answers with its pixel drawn, in Chrome and Edge 155. RULED A: the sandboxed-iframe host is the guard. Why: it passes the real-OOM row in a real browser, needs no native code, and the opaque origin cannot reach Tauri invoke, so the frame can write nothing. And the case that would change my mind (WebView2 not isolating sandboxed frames) is no worse than today: the export would die exactly as it does at b6969da, and E's 2a already keeps ELYSIUM out of OOM. So the residual is named and bounded, not blocking. Accepted beyond the named files, as needed: install/index.js, install.js (await build) and fromwords.js opts.onStage (ten stage calls, bytes pinned by row 1). C stands as an addition only (E's ArrayBuffer RangeError, by name); B is not built. For the final hand-back B owes: the probes re-run into logged files in the evidence folder; the run437.sh figures (touched files, export-isolated-mutation whole, and the core/close/xsec/mutation/cup harnesses as applied/caught/NOT APPLIED); commit by named paths; the map line. The page row's temp AC folder is not required; the 0 writes handed to native plus node rows 3/4 meet byte-identical. For the landing pane: merge with E's f2cd310 and D432, then re-run ELYSIUM's page Export once through both (B's route took 118.4 s without E's soup fix; E's took 34.9 s on the page) and name the time. surfaced +1.
